I still feel like I’m running on a low battery before the day even gets going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I have. For a long time now, there's just been this... gray, flat feeling most days. Even the things I used to really look forward to, like going fishing, tending the tomatoes in the yard, or going to a church concert, they just feel like items on a checklist now instead of something I actually enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: No, no I haven't. I mean, I know I just said everything feels a bit flat, but... I don't think about giving up or anything like that. It's really just that I'm tired of going through the motions. But no, I definitely haven't had any thoughts like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this gray, flat way?\n\nPatient: Well, let me think... it's been going on since I was pulled back into full operational leadership at work. That was about two years and four months ago. So, yeah, it's been a steady thing since right around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this gray, flat mood is mainly because of being pulled back into full operational leadership at work?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. For a long time now, there's just been this gray, flat feeling almost every day. It's gotten to the point where even the things I used to really look forward to—like going fishing, tending the tomatoes, or going to a church concert—they just feel like items on a checklist instead of something I actually enjoy doing.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you been feeling bad about yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel bad about myself. I know I do my job well, and I take care of my responsibilities... it's just that everything feels flat. After that, I just lie there until the alarm goes off. I mean, I can still focus on my work and get through all the technical stuff just fine. But I definitely don't feel like there's no hope or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot, even when there isn’t an immediate problem to deal with?\n\nPatient: No, not really. But then I end up waking up around four in the morning, and my mind just immediately starts sorting through server issues or compliance deadlines for work. After that, I just lie there until the alarm goes off. So by the time I actually get up, it feels like the night didn't really count.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any problems there. I mean, I can still focus on my work and get through all the technical stuff just fine. My concentration seems about the same as it always has been.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say hopeless. I mean, I know things will keep moving forward, and the work will get done. It's just... well, like I mentioned earlier, everything just feels kind of flat. But I definitely don't feel like there's no hope or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot, even when there isn’t an immediate problem to deal with?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I have the usual work stuff on my mind—like those compliance deadlines I mentioned—but those are actual things I have to handle. I don't really find myself just... worrying out of nowhere if there isn't a specific problem in front of me.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, has there been any stretch of two months or longer when this gray, flat mood went away completely?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just been a constant... well, just this constant flat feeling. I don't think there's been any time, definitely not a couple of months, where it just completely went away. It's pretty much always there in the background.\n\nDoctor: Have you had low energy nearly every day during this time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's mostly why I decided to come in. I'm just tired in a way that doesn't really make sense. I mean, I can get a full night's sleep, and I keep up with everything at work, but... I still feel like I'm running on a low battery before the day even gets going. And it's pretty much every day like that.\n\nDoctor: Has this waking up around four in the morning been happening at least three nights a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely at least three nights a week. I mean, falling asleep isn't really the problem. I usually drift off all right. But then, right around four, I'm just... awake. My brain just starts up, already sorting through server issues or those compliance deadlines I mentioned. And I just lie there until the alarm goes off. It honestly feels like the night didn't really count as sleep.\n\nDoctor: Does waking up around four in the morning leave you noticeably impaired during the day?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm still getting the essential work done, so I wouldn't say I'm completely impaired. But it definitely takes a toll. I mean, I've missed some routine appointments lately. And at home, I've just... let the garden go completely. I've also backed out of some church and community things that I used to be pretty reliable for. I just don't have the energy for them by the time I get through the workday.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when the depression was noticeably worse than usual?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's... it's been pretty consistent, honestly. Just this steady, low-grade drag. I haven't noticed any specific time where it suddenly got a lot worse for a couple of weeks or anything like that. It just stays about the same.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up around four in the morning, are you unable to fall back asleep even if you stay in bed and try?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. I stay in bed and try, but... no, I can't fall back asleep. Once my eyes open at four, that's pretty much it. I'll just lie there trying to quiet my mind and force it, but it just doesn't work. I mean, I can sleep fine, I keep up with things at work, but... I still feel like I’m running on a low battery before the day even gets going.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling down or depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I suppose I have. For a long time now, there's just been this... gray, flat feeling most days. Even the things I used to really look forward to, like going fishing, tending the tomatoes in the yard, or going to a church concert, they just feel like items on a checklist now instead of something I actually enjoy.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts that life isn’t worth living?\n\nPatient: No, no I haven't. I mean, I know I just said everything feels a bit flat, but... I don't think about giving up or anything like that. It's really just that I'm tired of going through the motions. But no, I definitely haven't had any thoughts like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this gray, flat way?\n\nPatient: Well, let me think... it's been going on since I was pulled back into full operational leadership at work. That was about two years and four months ago. So, yeah, it's been a steady thing since right around then.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel this gray, flat mood is mainly because of being pulled back into full operational leadership at work?\n\nPatient: I'm not entirely sure, to be honest. The timing definitely lines up with when I had to take that role back over, but... I don't know if the job itself is the main reason. It just sort of started around then and never really lifted.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, have you felt this gray, flat mood most days?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I'd say it's been most days. For a long time now, there's just been this gray, flat feeling almost every day. It's gotten to the point where even the things I used to really look forward to—like going fishing, tending the tomatoes, or going to a church concert—they just feel like items on a checklist instead of something I actually enjoy doing.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you been feeling bad about yourself?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I don't feel bad about myself. I know I do my job well, and I take care of my responsibilities... it's just that everything feels flat. But I don't feel worthless or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up in the morning, do you usually feel rested?\n\nPatient: Not exactly. I mean, I usually fall asleep all right when I first go to bed. But then I end up waking up around four in the morning, and my mind just immediately starts sorting through server issues or compliance deadlines for work. After that, I just lie there until the alarm goes off. So by the time I actually get up, it feels like the night didn't really count.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having trouble concentrating?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really noticed any problems there. I mean, I can still focus on my work and get through all the technical stuff just fine. My concentration seems about the same as it always has been.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling hopeless?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say hopeless. I mean, I know things will keep moving forward, and the work will get done. It's just... well, like I mentioned earlier, everything just feels kind of flat. But I definitely don't feel like there's no hope or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying a lot, even when there isn’t an immediate problem to deal with?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I mean, I have the usual work stuff on my mind—like those compliance deadlines I mentioned—but those are actual things I have to handle. I don't really find myself just... worrying out of nowhere if there isn't a specific problem in front of me.\n\nDoctor: During these past two years, has there been any stretch of two months or longer when this gray, flat mood went away completely?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's just been a constant... well, just this constant flat feeling. I don't think there's been any time, definitely not a couple of months, where it just completely went away. It's pretty much always there in the background.\n\nDoctor: Have you had low energy nearly every day during this time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's mostly why I decided to come in. I'm just tired in a way that doesn't really make sense. I mean, I can get a full night's sleep, and I keep up with everything at work, but... I still feel like I'm running on a low battery before the day even gets going. And it's pretty much every day like that.\n\nDoctor: Has this waking up around four in the morning been happening at least three nights a week?\n\nPatient: Yeah, it's definitely at least three nights a week. I mean, falling asleep isn't really the problem. I usually drift off all right. But then, right around four, I'm just... awake. My brain just starts up, already sorting through server issues or those compliance deadlines I mentioned. And I just lie there until the alarm goes off. It honestly feels like the night didn't really count as sleep.\n\nDoctor: Does waking up around four in the morning leave you noticeably impaired during the day?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm still getting the essential work done, so I wouldn't say I'm completely impaired. But it definitely takes a toll. I mean, I've missed some routine appointments lately. And at home, I've just... let the garden go completely. I've also backed out of some church and community things that I used to be pretty reliable for. I just don't have the energy for them by the time I get through the workday.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when the depression was noticeably worse than usual?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's... it's been pretty consistent, honestly. Just this steady, low-grade drag. I haven't noticed any specific time where it suddenly got a lot worse for a couple of weeks or anything like that. It just stays about the same.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up around four in the morning, are you unable to fall back asleep even if you stay in bed and try?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. I stay in bed and try, but... no, I can't fall back asleep. Once my eyes open at four, that's pretty much it. I'll just lie there trying to quiet my mind and force it, but it just doesn't work. I'm awake for good at that point.\n\nDoctor: Did the waking up at four in the morning start before the gray, flat mood began?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that, to be honest. It... they both kind of blur together in my mind at this point. I couldn't tell you for certain if the waking up started before the mood did. It feels like they've just both been going on for a long time now, so it's hard to separate which one actually came first.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. I mean, falling asleep isn't really the problem. I usually drift off all right. But then, right around four, I'm just... awake. My brain just starts up, already sorting through server issues or those compliance deadlines I mentioned. And I just lie there until the alarm goes off. It honestly feels like the night didn't really count as sleep.\n\nDoctor: Does waking up around four in the morning leave you noticeably impaired during the day?\n\nPatient: Well, I'm still getting the essential work done, so I wouldn't say I'm completely impaired. But it definitely takes a toll. I mean, I've missed some routine appointments lately. And at home, I've just... let the garden go completely. I've also backed out of some church and community things that I used to be pretty reliable for. I just don't have the energy for them by the time I get through the workday.\n\nDoctor: During this time, have you ever had a period of at least two weeks when the depression was noticeably worse than usual?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's... it's been pretty consistent, honestly. Just this steady, low-grade drag. I haven't noticed any specific time where it suddenly got a lot worse for a couple of weeks or anything like that. It just stays about the same.\n\nDoctor: When you wake up around four in the morning, are you unable to fall back asleep even if you stay in bed and try?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. I stay in bed and try, but... no, I can't fall back asleep. Once my eyes open at four, that's pretty much it. I'll just lie there trying to quiet my mind and force it, but it just doesn't work. I'm awake for good at that point.\n\nDoctor: Did the waking up at four in the morning start before the gray, flat mood began?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure about that, to be honest. It... they both kind of blur together in my mind at this point. I couldn't tell you for certain if the waking up started before the mood did. It feels like they've just both been going on for a long time now, so it's hard to separate which one actually came first.\n\nDoctor: On nights when you sleep through until morning, do you still feel that same gray, flat mood the next day?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I... I don't actually remember the last time I slept all the way through until morning. It's been quite a while.
